B.1 anihpj Dockerfile (Multi-Stage)

dockerfile
# ===== STAGE 1: Build dependencies =====
FROM python:3.11-slim AS builder
WORKDIR /app
COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --user --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

# ===== STAGE 2: Production image =====
FROM python:3.11-slim
RUN groupadd -r anihpj && useradd -r -g anihpj anihpj
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=builder /root/.local /home/anihpj/.local
COPY . .
RUN python manage.py collectstatic --noinput
RUN chown -R anihpj:anihpj /app
USER anihpj
EXPOSE 8000
HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s CMD curl -f http://localhost:8000/health || exit 1
CMD ["gunicorn", "anihpj.wsgi:application", "--bind", "0.0.0.0:8000", "--workers", "4"]

B.2 docker-compose.yml (Full Stack)

yaml — docker-compose.yml
version: '3.8'
services:
  django:
    build: .
    ports: ["8000:8000"]
    environment:
      - DB_HOST=postgres
      - REDIS_HOST=redis
      - DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=anihpj.settings
    depends_on: [postgres, redis]

  postgres:
    image: postgres:15
    environment:
      POSTGRES_DB: anihpj
      POSTGRES_USER: anihpj
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: secret
    volumes: [postgres_data:/var/lib/postgresql/data]

  redis:
    image: redis:7-alpine

  prometheus:
    image: prom/prometheus
    ports: ["9090:9090"]
    volumes: [./monitoring/prometheus.yml:/etc/prometheus/prometheus.yml]

  grafana:
    image: grafana/grafana
    ports: ["3000:3000"]
    environment:
      GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD: admin
      GF_INSTALL_PLUGINS: grafana-clock-panel
    volumes:
      - grafana_data:/var/lib/grafana
      - ./monitoring/grafana/datasources:/etc/grafana/provisioning/datasources
      - ./monitoring/grafana/dashboards:/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards

  loki:
    image: grafana/loki:latest
    ports: ["3100:3100"]
    command: -config.file=/etc/loki/loki-config.yaml
    volumes: [./monitoring/loki-config.yaml:/etc/loki/loki-config.yaml]

  promtail:
    image: grafana/promtail:latest
    volumes:
      - /var/log:/var/log:ro
      - ./monitoring/promtail-config.yaml:/etc/promtail/config.yml

volumes:
  postgres_data:
  grafana_data:

B.3 Kubernetes Deployment YAML

yaml — k8s/anihpj-deployment.yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: anihpj
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  replicas: 3
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: anihpj
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: anihpj
      annotations:
        prometheus.io/scrape: "true"
        prometheus.io/port: "8000"
        prometheus.io/path: "/metrics"
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: django
        image: anihpj/anihpj:latest
        ports:
        - containerPort: 8000
        env:
        - name: DB_HOST
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: anihpj-secrets
              key: db-host
        - name: DB_PASSWORD
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: anihpj-secrets
              key: db-password
        resources:
          requests: {memory: "256Mi", cpu: "250m"}
          limits: {memory: "512Mi", cpu: "500m"}
        livenessProbe:
          httpGet: {path: /health, port: 8000}
          initialDelaySeconds: 30
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet: {path: /health, port: 8000}
          initialDelaySeconds: 5
---

  namespace: anihpj

  - port: 80
    targetPort: 8000

B.6 Loki Promtail DaemonSet

yaml — k8s/promtail-daemonset.yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: DaemonSet
metadata:
  name: promtail
  namespace: monitoring
spec:
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: promtail
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: promtail
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: promtail
        image: grafana/promtail:latest
        args:
        - -config.file=/etc/promtail/config.yml
        volumeMounts:
        - name: varlog
          mountPath: /var/log
          readOnly: true
        - name: config
          mountPath: /etc/promtail
      volumes:
      - name: varlog
        hostPath:
          path: /var/log
      - name: config
        configMap:
          name: promtail-config

📎 Appendix C: Grafana Provisioning Files

Appendix C: Infrastructure as Code for Grafana

Complete provisioning YAML files for data sources, dashboards, alert rules, and notification templates. Ready to deploy with GitOps.

📖 How to Use This Appendix

Place these files in your Grafana provisioning directory (typically /etc/grafana/provisioning/). Grafana auto-loads them on startup and watches for changes. Provisioned resources are read-only in the UI — edit the YAML files, not the UI. This is the foundation of GitOps for Grafana: dashboards, data sources, and alerts stored in Git, deployed via CI/CD.

C.1 Data Source Provisioning

# provisioning/datasources/all.yaml
apiVersion: 1
datasources:
  - name: Prometheus
    type: prometheus
    url: http://prometheus:9090
    access: proxy
    isDefault: true
    editable: false
  - name: Loki
    type: loki
    url: http://loki:3100
    access: proxy
  - name: PostgreSQL-anihpj
    type: postgres
    url: postgres:5432
    database: anihpj
    user: grafana_reader
    secureJsonData:
      password: ${PG_PASSWORD}
    editable: false

C.2 Dashboard Provisioning

# provisioning/dashboards/anihpj.yaml
apiVersion: 1
providers:
  - name: anihpj
    folder: anihpj
    type: file
    options:
      path: /etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/anihpj

C.3 Alert Rule Provisioning

yaml — provisioning/alerting/anihpj-alerts.yaml
apiVersion: 1
groups:
  - orgId: 1
    name: anihpj-alerts
    folder: anihpj
    interval: 1m
    rules:
      - uid: high-error-rate
        title: "anihpj High Error Rate"
        condition: C
        data:
          - refId: A
            datasourceUid: prometheus
            model:
              expr: sum(rate(django_http_requests_total{status=~"5.."}[5m]))
          - refId: B
            datasourceUid: prometheus
            model:
              expr: sum(rate(django_http_requests_total[5m]))
          - refId: C
            datasourceUid: __expr__
            model:
              type: math
              expression: $A / $B > 0.05
        noDataState: NoData
        execErrState: Error
        for: 2m
        annotations:
          summary: "anihpj error rate {{ humanizePercentage $values.C.Value }}"
          runbook_url: "https://wiki.anihpj.com/runbooks/high-error-rate"
        labels:
          severity: critical
          team: sre

      - uid: p95-latency-spike
        title: "anihpj P95 Latency Spike"
        condition: A
        data:
          - refId: A
            datasourceUid: prometheus
            model:
              expr: histogram_quantile(0.95, sum(rate(request_duration_bucket[5m])) by (le)) > 2
        for: 5m
        annotations:
          summary: "P95 latency > 2s on {{ $labels.endpoint }}"
        labels:
          severity: warning

📎 Appendix D: Quick Reference Cards

Appendix D: Print-Friendly Quick Reference Cards

Six comprehensive cheat sheets covering PromQL, LogQL, Grafana CLI, Grafana API, SQL Macros, and Panel Decision Matrix. Designed for last-minute review before the exam. Print these pages and keep them on your desk during study sessions.

📎 Reference📝 Cheat Sheet🏆 Exam
📖 How to Use These Cards

These are quick reference cards — not tutorials. Use them: (1) During study as a quick lookup, (2) For last-minute review before the exam, (3) As mental models to internalize patterns. Exam tip: If you can recognize these patterns instantly, you'll answer 20-30% of exam questions immediately without hesitation.

D.1 PromQL Cheat Sheet

PromQL — Essential Reference
# === RATE FUNCTIONS (use with counters ONLY) ===
rate(metric[5m])            # Per-second rate of increase (smooth, best for graphs)
irate(metric[5m])           # Instant rate (higher resolution, more sensitive)
increase(metric[1h])        # Total increase over time range
delta(metric[5m])           # Difference between first & last (gauges only)

# === AGGREGATION OPERATORS ===
sum by (label) (query)      # Sum grouped by label
avg by (label) (query)      # Average grouped by label
max by (label) (query)      # Maximum grouped by label
min by (label) (query)      # Minimum grouped by label
count by (label) (query)    # Count of series grouped by label
topk(N, query)              # Top N largest values
bottomk(N, query)           # Bottom N smallest values

# === HISTOGRAM FUNCTIONS ===
histogram_quantile(0.95, sum(rate(bucket[5m])) by (le))  # P95
histogram_quantile(0.99, sum(rate(bucket[5m])) by (le))  # P99
# ⚠️ MUST aggregate by (le) BEFORE histogram_quantile()

# === LABEL MATCHING ===
{label="value"}             # Exact match
{label=~"regex.*"}          # Regex match (=~)
{label!~"regex.*"}          # Not regex (!~)
{label!="value"}            # Not equal
{label=~"prod|staging"}     # Multiple values via regex

# === VECTOR MATCHING ===
on(label) queryA / queryB   # Match on specific label
ignoring(label)             # Match on everything EXCEPT label
group_left / group_right    # Many-to-one matching

# === SPECIAL ===
absent(metric)              # Returns 1 if metric doesn't exist (dead monitoring alert!)
absent_over_time(metric[5m]) # No data in time window
offset 1d                   # Query data from 1 day ago (comparisons)
@ 1609459200                # Query at specific Unix timestamp

D.2 LogQL Cheat Sheet

LogQL — Essential Reference
# === STREAM SELECTORS ===
{job="app"}                 # Select log streams by label
{job="app", env="prod"}    # Multiple labels (AND logic)
{job=~"app.*"}              # Label regex match

# === LINE FILTERS (applied after stream selector) ===
|= "text"                   # Contains (keep lines with "text")
!= "text"                   # Not contains (remove lines with "text")
|~ "regex"                  # Regex match (keep matching lines)
!~ "regex"                  # Not regex (remove matching lines)

# === LOG PIPELINES (parse + transform) ===
| json                      # Parse JSON log lines into fields
| logfmt                    # Parse logfmt (key=value) format
| pattern "<ip> - <user>"  # Extract fields by named pattern
| regexp "(?P<status>\\d+)"  # Extract fields by regex
| unpack                    # Unpack packed fields
| line_format "{{.msg}}"    # Reformat output line
| label_format level=UPPER   # Transform label values

# === METRIC QUERIES (generate numbers from logs) ===
rate({job="app"}[5m])       # Log entries per second
count_over_time({job="app"}[5m])       # Count logs in time range
bytes_rate({job="app"}[5m])            # Bytes per second
bytes_over_time({job="app"}[5m])       # Total bytes
sum by (level) (count_over_time({}[5m])) # Aggregation

# === UNWRAP (extract numeric values from parsed fields) ===
| json | unwrap duration
| quantile_over_time(0.95, duration [5m])  # P95 from log values

D.3 Grafana CLI Cheat Sheet

grafana-cli — Essential Commands
# === PLUGIN MANAGEMENT ===
grafana-cli plugins install <id>    # Install plugin (RESTART after!)
grafana-cli plugins ls                # List installed plugins
grafana-cli plugins list-remote       # Browse available plugins
grafana-cli plugins update <id>      # Update specific plugin
grafana-cli plugins update-all        # Update all plugins
grafana-cli plugins remove <id>      # Uninstall plugin

# === ADMIN OPERATIONS ===
grafana-cli admin reset-admin-password <new-password>
grafana-cli admin secrets migrate    # Re-encrypt secrets
grafana-cli admin settings migrate   # Migrate settings to new format

# === SERVER ===
grafana-server -config /path/grafana.ini   # Start with custom config
grafana-server -homepath /usr/share/grafana # Custom home path

# === DOCKER ===
docker run -e "GF_INSTALL_PLUGINS=plugin1,plugin2" grafana/grafana
# No restart needed — plugins installed BEFORE Grafana starts

# === ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES ===
GF_SERVER_HTTP_PORT=8080              # Override [server].http_port
GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD=secret     # Override [security].admin_password
GF_DATABASE_TYPE=postgres             # Override [database].type
GF_INSTALL_PLUGINS=id1,id2            # Auto-install plugins on start

# === SERVICE (Linux) ===
systemctl start grafana-server        # Start Grafana
systemctl restart grafana-server      # Restart (after plugin install!)
systemctl status grafana-server       # Check status
journalctl -u grafana-server -f       # Follow logs

D.4 Grafana API Cheat Sheet

Grafana HTTP API — Quick Reference
# Auth: Authorization: Bearer <service-account-token>
# Base: http://localhost:3000/api

# === DASHBOARDS ===
GET    /api/search?type=dash-db&query=anihpj     # Search dashboards
GET    /api/dashboards/uid/:uid                   # Get dashboard JSON
POST   /api/dashboards/db                         # Create/update dashboard
DELETE /api/dashboards/uid/:uid                   # Delete dashboard
GET    /api/dashboards/home                       # Get home dashboard

# === DATA SOURCES ===
GET    /api/datasources                            # List all
POST   /api/datasources                            # Create new
GET    /api/datasources/name/:name                 # Get by name
DELETE /api/datasources/:id                        # Delete

# === ANNOTATIONS ===
POST   /api/annotations                            # Create (CI/CD!)
GET    /api/annotations?tags=deploy                # Query by tag

# === ALERTING ===
GET    /api/alertmanager/grafana/api/v2/alerts     # List active alerts
GET    /api/alertmanager/grafana/api/v2/silences   # List silences

# === ADMIN ===
GET    /api/admin/stats                            # Server statistics
GET    /api/health                                 # Health check (no auth)

# === CURL EXAMPLES ===
curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" /api/search?type=dash-db
curl -X POST /api/annotations -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"text":"v2.1 deployed","tags":["deploy"]}'

D.5 SQL Macros Reference

SQL Macros — Every One You Need
# === TIME FILTER MACROS ===
$__timeFilter(column)
  → WHERE column BETWEEN '2024-01-01T00:00:00' AND '2024-01-01T23:59:59'
  # ⚠️ REQUIRED for any time-series SQL query. Without it = ALL data returned.

$__timeGroup(column, '1h')
  → GROUP BY floor(extract(epoch FROM column)/3600)*3600
  # Use for time series grouping. Returns Unix timestamp.

$__timeGroup(column, '1h', 0)
  → Same but fills gaps with 0 (NULL becomes 0)

# === TIME RANGE MACROS ===
$__timeFrom()
  → '2024-01-01T00:00:00'  # Start of dashboard time range

$__timeTo()
  → '2024-01-01T23:59:59'  # End of dashboard time range

# === UNIX EPOCH MACROS (for integer timestamp columns) ===
$__unixEpochFilter(column)
  → WHERE column >= 1704067200 AND column <= 1704153599

$__unixEpochGroup(column, '1h')
  → GROUP BY column - column % 3600

# === COMPLETE TIME SERIES QUERY PATTERN ===
SELECT
  $__timeGroup(created_at, '1h') AS time,
  COUNT(*) AS "Job Posts"
FROM jobpost_jobpost
WHERE $__timeFilter(created_at)
GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY 1

# === MACRO GOTCHAS ===
# ❌ Don't use $__timeFilter on non-time columns
# ❌ Don't forget $__timeFilter — it's the #4 exam mistake
# ✅ Always test macros with Query Inspector → Query tab

D.6 Panel Type Decision Matrix

Your Data / QuestionBest PanelWhy
Trend over time (metrics)📊 Time SeriesLine/bar/point visualization with thresholds
Single important number🔢 StatHero number with sparkline, color thresholds
Health / utilization (%)🎚️ GaugeSemi-circle or bar with color bands
Multi-instance status📊 Bar GaugeCompact horizontal/vertical bars
Detailed records📋 TableSortable, filterable, cell display modes
Parts of a whole🥧 Pie ChartDistribution (max ~8 slices)
Value distribution📶 HistogramBucket frequency, no time axis
Bucketed over time🟧 HeatmapColor intensity over time bins
Discrete state changes⏱️ State TimelineDeployments, pod states, alert states
Service dependencies🕸️ Node GraphArchitecture topology, call chains
Geographic data🗺️ GeomapLat/lon on interactive map
Documentation / notes📝 Text/MarkdownRich text, runbook links, team info
Live log stream📜 LogsIn-dashboard Loki/ES log tail
⚡ Exam Speed Tip

When asked "Which panel for X?", first identify if your data has TIME on the X-axis. If YES → Time Series, State Timeline, or Heatmap. If NO → Stat, Gauge, Pie, Histogram, Table, Geomap. Time-axis questions are the most common panel selection traps on the exam.

📎 Appendix E: Exam Practice — Mock Tests

Appendix E: Five Full-Length Mock Exams with Sample Questions

Simulate the real exam experience with five domain-focused mock exams. Each includes sample questions with answers and explanations to give you a taste of the real exam format. For the full 250+ question bank, see every chapter's Practice Questions section.

📎 Reference📝 Practice🏆 Exam
📖 How to Use These Mocks

Do NOT take these until you've completed ALL chapters. Use them as finals: Mock 1 after finishing Parts 1-2. Mock 2 after Parts 1-2. Mock 3 after Part 3-4. Mock 4 after Part 5-6. Mock 5 LAST — simulate real exam (90 min, no notes, no phone). Target: 75%+ on Mock 5 before scheduling the real exam.

E.1 Mock Exam 1: Fundamentals Focus (Chapters 1-3)

30 questions | 45 minutes | Domain: Fundamentals (15% of real exam)

Sample Q1

Where does Grafana store your monitoring data (metrics, logs, traces)?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Nowhere. Grafana does NOT store monitoring data. It stores only configuration in its internal database. Metrics/data stay in external systems (Prometheus, Loki, PostgreSQL).

💡 EXPLANATION

This is the #1 misconception tested on the exam. Grafana is the visualization LAYER, not the storage layer. It queries external data sources via plugins in real-time. If an answer says "Grafana stores metrics in its database" — it's wrong.

Sample Q2

Which environment variable changes the default HTTP port from 3000 to 8080?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

GF_SERVER_HTTP_PORT=8080. Pattern: GF_<SECTION>_<KEY>.

💡 EXPLANATION

The GF_ prefix + uppercase section + uppercase key pattern works for EVERY grafana.ini setting. [security].admin_password → GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD. This is tested on the exam — know this pattern cold.

Review Chapters 1-3 practice questions (30 Qs) for the full mock experience.

E.2 Mock Exam 2: Data Sources & Queries (Chapters 4-7)

40 questions | 60 minutes | Domain: Data Sources (20% of real exam)

Sample Q1

What's wrong with this PromQL: rate(http_requests_total)?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Missing range vector [time]. Correct: rate(http_requests_total[5m]). rate() requires a range to calculate the per-second rate.

💡 EXPLANATION

This is the #2 most common PromQL mistake. rate() needs to see HOW the counter changed over time. Without [5m], it gets only one value and can't calculate a rate. The error message literally says "expected type range vector" — read error messages!

Sample Q2

Which SQL macro adds time-based filtering to a query?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

$__timeFilter(column) — adds WHERE column BETWEEN 'start' AND 'end' based on the dashboard time range.

💡 EXPLANATION

Forgetting $__timeFilter() is the #4 exam mistake (Ch25). Without it, your SQL query returns ALL data regardless of dashboard time range. Always include it in time-series SQL queries.

Review Chapters 4-7 practice questions (40 Qs) for the full mock experience.

E.3 Mock Exam 3: Dashboards & Alerting (Chapters 8-14)

50 questions | 75 minutes | Domains: Dashboards (25%) + Alerting (15%) = 40% of real exam

Sample Q1

Which panel type is best for showing parts of a whole (e.g., status code distribution)?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Pie Chart / Donut. Best for distribution with max 6-8 slices. For more categories, use a Bar Chart or Table.

💡 EXPLANATION

Panel selection rule: First ask "Does my data have TIME on the X-axis?" If NO → Stat, Gauge, Pie, Histogram, Table, Geomap. Pie Chart = parts of a whole, no time dimension. This is a 25%-weight exam domain question.

Sample Q2

What is the difference between a Contact Point and a Notification Policy?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Contact Point = WHERE (Slack, email). Notification Policy = WHICH alerts go WHERE (routing rules).

💡 EXPLANATION

This is the #9 exam mistake. Confusing these two is very common. Contact Point = destination (phone number). Notification Policy = routing (if Mom calls → ring, if spam → voicemail).

Review Chapters 8-14 practice questions (70 Qs) for the full mock experience.

E.4 Mock Exam 4: Admin & Advanced (Chapters 15-20)

30 questions | 45 minutes | Domain: Admin (15%) + Advanced troubleshooting

Sample Q1

How do you backup a Grafana instance for disaster recovery?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

1) Backup database (pg_dump for PostgreSQL), 2) Backup file system (plugins, provisioning), 3) Export dashboards via API. Full DB dump + provisioning Git repo = complete DR plan.

💡 EXPLANATION

The database stores: dashboards, users, orgs, data sources, API keys. The file system stores: plugins, provisioning configs, custom images. Dashboard JSON export is a SUPPLEMENT, not replacement for database backup. See Ch17 for full DR plan.

Sample Q2

Which Query Inspector tab shows raw response data?

Click to reveal answer
✅ ANSWER

Data tab. Shows every data point returned by the query. Stats tab shows timing. Query tab shows the sent query.

💡 EXPLANATION

Query Inspector tabs: Query = what was sent, Data = what came back, Stats = how long it took, JSON = full raw payload, Error = what went wrong. Access: Panel menu → Inspect → Query. This is your #1 debugging tool.

Review Chapters 15-20 practice questions (50 Qs) for the full mock experience.

  1. ⏱️ Set a 90-minute timer — no pausing
  2. 📵 No phone, no notes, no other tabs open

       Pass 1 (0-30 min): Sweep — answer all easy Qs

📎 Appendix F: Troubleshooting Flowcharts

Appendix F: Visual Troubleshooting Guides for On-Call Engineers

Step-by-step diagnostic flowcharts for the five most common Grafana production issues. Print these and keep them handy during on-call shifts. Each flowchart follows a systematic "check this → if yes → check that" pattern.

📎 Reference🔧 Debug🚨 On-Call
📖 How to Use These Flowcharts

These are decision trees for on-call debugging. Start at the top and follow the arrows. Each decision point has a YES (→) and NO (→) path. Pro tip: Time-box each diagnostic step to 2 minutes. If you haven't found the issue after following the entire flowchart, escalate to a senior engineer. These cover 80% of common issues.

F.1 "Dashboard Shows No Data" — Decision Tree

🔍 DASHBOARD "NO DATA" — Systematic Diagnosis
  START ──▶ ⏱️ Is the dashboard time range correct?
  │          ├─ NO → Adjust time picker. Refresh. Fixed? ✅
  │          └─ YES → Continue ↓
  │
  ▼
  🔌 Is the data source healthy?
  │  (Connections → Data Sources → Save & Test)
  ├─ NO (red X) → Check: is the data source running? Correct URL/port? Network?
  └─ YES (green ✓) → Continue ↓
  │
  ▼
  🔍 Query Inspector → Query Tab
  │  └─ Copy query → paste into Explore → Does it return data?
  │     ├─ YES → Problem is PANEL configuration:
  │     │         • Check transformations (filtering out data?)
  │     │         • Check panel repeat variable
  │     │         • Check field overrides (series hidden?)
  │     └─ NO → Problem is QUERY or DATA SOURCE:
  │              • Verify labels match actual metrics
  │              • Check variable values ($env = "prod" but data has env="production"?)
  │              • Check metric exists (use Explore to browse metrics)
  │              • PromQL: Is it a counter? Use rate() not raw.
  │              • SQL: Forgot $__timeFilter()?

  🎯 80% OF "NO DATA" = wrong time range OR label mismatch

F.2 "Alert Not Firing" — Diagnosis Flowchart

🔔 ALERT NOT FIRING — Systematic Diagnosis
  START ──▶ 📊 Check Alert Rule State (Alerting → Alert Rules → click rule)
  │
  ├─ State = "Normal" → Condition is NOT met
  │   └─ Test the query in Explore. Does it return data? Does it cross the threshold?
  │      ├─ Query wrong → Fix query
  │      └─ Query correct but threshold too high → Lower threshold
  │
  ├─ State = "Pending" → Condition IS met but "Evaluate For" hasn't elapsed
  │   └─ Wait for the "for" duration (e.g., 5m). This is NORMAL behavior.
  │      Alert WILL fire after the pending period. Reduce "for" if too slow.
  │
  ├─ State = "Firing" (but no notification received) →
  │   └─ Check Notification Policy:
  │      • Does the alert's labels MATCH the policy's label matchers?
  │      • Is there a nested policy overriding the root?
  │      • Is "Continue matching" configured correctly?
  │   └─ Check Contact Point:
  │      • Test notification (Contact Points → Test)
  │      • Check webhook URL is valid (Slack channel still exists?)
  │      • Check SMTP settings (grafana.ini [smtp])
  │
  ├─ State = "Error" → Query execution failed
  │   └─ Query Inspector → Error tab → read error message → fix query
  │
  └─ 🔇 Check Active Silences:
      • Alerting → Silences → any matching labels silencing this alert?
      • Check: label matchers, time range, created by whom?

  🎯 MOST COMMON: Notification policy label mismatch OR silence is active

F.3 "Query Returns Errors" — Debug Flowchart

❌ QUERY ERRORS — Error Message Decoder
  START ──▶ 📋 Read the error message CAREFULLY (Query Inspector → Error tab)

  ┌─ "expected type range vector in eval"
  │  → Using rate()/increase() without [5m] → Add [range]:
  │    rate(metric) ❌ → rate(metric[5m]) ✅
  │
  ├─ "connection refused"
  │  → Data source is DOWN → Check: docker ps, systemctl status, ping host
  │    Grafana can't reach the data source — network/firewall/DNS issue
  │
  ├─ "authentication failed" / "401 Unauthorized"
  │  → Wrong credentials → Check username/password, API token expiry
  │    For cloud data sources: check IAM roles, access keys
  │
  ├─ "syntax error" / "parse error"
  │  → Query has a typo → Copy to text editor, check brackets, commas, quotes
  │    PromQL: missing comma in label selector? {job="app",env="prod"}
  │    SQL: missing FROM clause? unclosed parentheses?
  │
  ├─ "Too many time series" / "query timeout"
  │  → Query is too broad → Add label filters: {job="anihpj"} not {}
  │    Reduce time range. Use recording rules. Increase max data points.
  │
  ├─ "no such file or directory" (Grafana server logs)
  │  → Missing plugin or provisioning file → Check file paths in grafana.ini
  │
  └─ GENERIC: Simplify query → add parts back incrementally → find broken part

  🎯 GOLDEN RULE: The error message ALWAYS tells you what's wrong. Read it.

F.4 "Grafana Won't Start" — Recovery Steps

🚫 GRAFANA WON'T START — Recovery Steps
  START ──▶ 📋 Check logs: docker logs grafana OR journalctl -u grafana-server

  ┌─ "database is locked"
  │  → SQLite issue with concurrent access → Switch to PostgreSQL/MySQL
  │    SQLite doesn't support concurrent writes. Multiple Grafana instances?
  │
  ├─ "bind: address already in use" / "port already in use"
  │  → Port 3000 occupied → netstat -tlnp | grep 3000 → kill or change port
  │    GF_SERVER_HTTP_PORT=8080
  │
  ├─ "permission denied"
  │  → Wrong file ownership → chown -R grafana:grafana /var/lib/grafana
  │    Check data, logs, plugins, and provisioning directory permissions
  │
  ├─ "plugin incompatible" / "plugin not found"
  │  → Plugin version doesn't match Grafana version → Update plugin
  │    Remove problematic plugin: grafana-cli plugins remove <id>
  │    Start with --disable-plugins flag for emergency recovery
  │
  ├─ "syntax error in grafana.ini"
  │  → Check config file: grafana-server -config /etc/grafana/grafana.ini -validate
  │    Common: missing closing bracket, wrong section name, extra semicolons
  │
  ├─ "could not open database" (PostgreSQL)
  │  → PostgreSQL not running or wrong credentials → Check pg_isready
  │    Verify: host, port, database, user, password in [database] section
  │
  └─ "no space left on device"
      → Disk full → df -h → clean old data/logs → increase disk

  🎯 EMERGENCY: grafana-server -config /dev/null (starts with defaults, bypasses bad config)

F.5 "Data Source Connection Failed" — Troubleshooting Guide

🔌 DATA SOURCE CONNECTION FAILED — Network & Config Diagnosis
  START ──▶ 🏥 Is the data source RUNNING?
  │          docker ps | grep prometheus  OR  systemctl status prometheus
  │          ├─ NOT RUNNING → Start it → Retry Save & Test
  │          └─ RUNNING → Continue ↓
  │
  ▼
  🌐 Can Grafana REACH the data source on the network?
  │  docker exec grafana ping prometheus  (Docker)
  │  OR  curl http://prometheus:9090  (from Grafana server)
  ├─ CANNOT REACH → Network issue:
  │   • Docker: are they on the same network? (docker network ls)
  │   • K8s: correct Service name? Correct namespace? NetworkPolicy?
  │   • Firewall: port open? Security group allowing traffic?
  └─ CAN REACH → Continue ↓
  │
  ▼
  🔗 Correct URL and PORT?
  │  Prometheus: http://prometheus:9090  (default port 9090)
  │  Loki: http://loki:3100              (default port 3100)
  │  PostgreSQL: postgres:5432           (default port 5432)
  ├─ WRONG → Fix URL/port → Retry Save & Test
  └─ CORRECT → Continue ↓
  │
  ▼
  🔐 Authentication issue?
  │  • Basic Auth: username/password correct?
  │  • TLS: using https but cert is self-signed? → "Skip TLS Verify" for testing
  │  • Cloud data sources: IAM roles, access keys, service accounts valid?
  │  • Grafana logs: grep "datasource" /var/log/grafana/grafana.log
  │
  ▼
  🎯 Save & Test → GREEN CHECK ✅ = WORKING!

🔧 Troubleshooting Quick Reference — Top 10 Fixes

#SymptomMost Likely FixFirst Command
1Dashboard "No data"Check time range + label matchQuery Inspector → Data tab
2Alert not firingCheck notification policy labelsAlerting → Alert Rules → state
3"expected range vector"Add [5m] to rate()rate(metric[5m])
4"connection refused"Data source is downdocker ps \| grep prometheus
5Grafana won't startCheck logs for specific errordocker logs grafana
6Port 3000 in useChange port or kill processGF_SERVER_HTTP_PORT=8080
7Slow dashboardProfile with Stats tabQuery Inspector → Stats
8Plugin not appearingRestart Grafanasystemctl restart grafana-server
9SQL no time filterAdd $__timeFilter()WHERE $__timeFilter(col)
10Save & Test failsCheck network + auth chainSee F.5 flowchart above
